# Generate large CSV file and add make target

## User

<attached_files>
```file://~/code/rust-editor/Makefile

```
</attached_files>

Continuing work from thread T-019b274f-d35e-7014-9c1f-979b1452722a. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@Makefile

- I just implemented Phase 1 of the CSV viewer feature, which is now complete and working
- The CSV viewer is toggled via the command palette with "Toggle CSV View" command
- To test it, I need to open a CSV file in the editor and then toggle CSV mode
- The editor binary is built with `cargo run` or `cargo build --release`
- The Makefile already has targets like `make run` that I can follow as patterns
- Sample data files typically go in a tests/fixtures or similar directory, or could be generated on the fly

lets now generate a very large csv file to use as sample data and add a make target to open it with "make csv"

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive view of the CSV implementation. Let me compile the final list of all relevant files:

## Summary

Based on my search through the codebase, here are all the files related to CSV viewer functionality:

### Core CSV Module Files:
1. **`src/csv/mod.rs`** - Main CSV module with architecture documentation
2. **`src/csv/model.rs`** - Core data structures: `CsvState`, `CsvData`, `CellPosition`, `Delimiter`
3. **`src/csv/render.rs`** - CSV grid rendering with `CsvRenderLayout` and cell display logic
4. **`src/csv/navigation.rs`** - Cell navigation (arrow keys, Tab, Home, End, Page Up/Down)
5. **`src/csv/viewport.rs`** - Viewport calculations (`CsvViewport`) for scrolling and visible region tracking
6. **`src/csv/parser.rs`** - CSV parsing using the `csv` crate

### View/Rendering Files:
7. **`src/view/mod.rs`** - Contains `render_csv_grid()` function (lines 704-900+) that renders the complete CSV grid including:
   - Row number headers
   - Column letter headers
   - Grid lines (horizontal and vertical)
   - Cell content with text truncation
   - Number alignment (right-aligned for numbers)
   - Selected cell highlight with border
8. **`src/view/geometry.rs`** - Hit-testing and coordinate transformation helpers (contains click detection logic)

### Message & Command Files:
9. **`src/messages.rs`** - `CsvMsg` enum with all CSV navigation commands
10. **`src/keymap/command.rs`** - CSV-related command definitions (CsvToggle, CsvMoveUp, etc.)
11. **`src/commands.rs`** - Command registry including `ToggleCsvView`

### Update/State Management:
12. **`src/update/csv.rs`** - CSV message handlers and state transitions (toggle mode, cell navigation, viewport management)
13. **`src/model/editor.rs`** - Contains `ViewMode::Csv` enum variant and editor state methods

### Input & Event Handling:
14. **`src/runtime/app.rs`** - Mouse and keyboard event handling (lines 476+):
    - `WindowEvent::MouseInput` handlers for left/middle mouse clicks
    - Click detection and coordinate to cursor conversion
    - Double-click detection
15. **`src/runtime/input.rs`** - Keyboard input routing (currently text-mode focused, CSV input is keymap-based)

### Theme Files:
16. **`src/theme.rs`** - CSV theming: `CsvTheme`, `CsvThemeData` with colors for headers, grid lines, selected cells

### Documentation:
17. **`docs/feature/csv-editor.md`** - CSV feature documentation
18. **`docs/ROADMAP.md`** - Project roadmap mentioning CSV structures

### Test Data:
19. **`samples/large_data.csv`** - Sample CSV file for testing

**Note on Click Handling:** Currently, CSV cell clicks are NOT implemented. The mouse click handler in `src/runtime/app.rs` converts pixel coordinates to text line/column positions (via `pixel_to_cursor()` in `src/view/geometry.rs`), but this only applies to text mode. CSV mode would need a separate `pixel_to_cell()` function to convert clicks to cell coordinates, which is not yet implemented.

Based on my search through the codebase, here are the key files that contain hit testing code, mouse click handling, and input event processing:

## Primary Files for Click Handling and Hit Testing:

**1. `~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/app.rs`**
   - Main event handler with `handle_event()` function
   - Mouse click handling for left button (lines 476-629): single/double/triple click detection, drag selection
   - Middle-click handling (lines 641-685): rectangle selection
   - Click count tracking and modifier key detection (Shift, Alt/Option, Cmd)
   - Cursor icon updates based on hit region

**2. `~/code/rust-editor/src/view/geometry.rs`**
   - Core hit-testing functions:
     - `pixel_to_cursor()` (line 217) - converts pixel coordinates to line/column
     - `pixel_to_line_and_visual_column()` (line 253) - for rectangle selection
     - `pixel_to_cursor_in_group()` (line 283) - group-specific hit testing
     - `is_in_status_bar()` (line 167) - status bar detection
     - `is_in_group_tab_bar()` (line 183) - tab bar hit testing
     - `tab_at_position()` (line 192) - find clicked tab index
     - `is_in_modal()` (line 404) - modal dialog hit testing
   - Tab/character column conversion helpers

**3. `~/code/rust-editor/src/view/mod.rs`**
   - `Renderer` struct wrapper methods for hit testing:
     - `pixel_to_cursor()` (line 1453)
     - `pixel_to_line_and_visual_column()` (line 1461)
     - `is_in_status_bar()` (line 1473)
     - `tab_at_position()` (line 1481)

**4. `~/code/rust-editor/src/util/text.rs` and `~/code/rust-editor/src/view/geometry.rs`**
   - `visual_col_to_char_col()` - converts screen position to character index, accounting for tabs
   - `char_col_to_visual_col()` - inverse conversion

**5. `~/code/rust-editor/src/messages.rs`**
   - Click-related message definitions:
     - `SetCursorPosition` - from mouse click
     - `SelectWord` - double-click
     - `SelectLine` - triple-click
     - `ExtendSelectionToPosition` - Shift+Click
     - `ToggleCursorAtPosition` - Option/Alt+Click
     - `StartRectangleSelection` - middle-click
     - `UpdateRectangleSelection` - middle-click drag
     - `FinishRectangleSelection` - middle-click release

**6. `~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/input.rs`**
   - Keyboard input handler with modal routing
   - Option double-tap detection for multi-cursor

These files provide the complete pipeline from raw mouse events to cursor positioning and selection updates.

Memory-efficient storage using delimited strings instead of Vec<Vec<String>>.\n4: \n5: use super::viewport::CsvViewport;\n6: \n7: /// Internal delimiter for cell storage (0xFA - rarely used in real data)\n8: pub const CELL_DELIMITER: char = '\\u{00FA}';\n9: \n10: /// Supported CSV delimiters\n11: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]\n12: pub enum Delimiter {\n13: significant overhead,\n57: /// each row is stored as a single string with cells delimited by CELL_DELIMITER (0xFA).\n58: /// This reduces memory allocations while still allowing O(1) row access.\n59: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n60: pub struct CsvData {\n61:     /// Each row stored as delimiter-separated string\n62:     rows: Vec<String>,\n63:     /// Number of columns (max across all rows)\n64:     column_count: usize,\n65: }\n66: \n67: impl CsvData {\n68:     /// Create empty CSV data\n69:     pub fn new() -> Self {\n70:         Self::default()\n71:     }\n72: \n73:     /// Create CSV data from parsed rows\n74:     pub fn from_rows(parsed_rows: Vec<Vec<String>>) -> Self {\n75:         let column_count = parsed_rows.iter().map(|r| r.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n76: \n77:         let rows = parsed_rows\n78:             .into_iter()\n79:             .map(|row| row.join(&CELL_DELIMITER.to_string()))\n80:             .collect();\n81: \n82:         Self { rows, column_count }\n83:     }\n84: \n85:     /// Get number of rows\n86:     pub fn row_count(&self) -> usize {\n87:         self.rows.len()\n88:     }\n89: \n90:     /// Get number of columns\n91:     pub fn column_count(&self) -> usize {\n92:         self.column_count\n93:     }\n94: \n95:     /// Get cell value at position\n96:     pub fn get(&self, row: usize, col: usize) -> &str {\n97:         if row >= self.rows.len() {\n98:             return \"\";\n99:         }\n100: \n101:         let row_str = &self.rows[row];\n102:         let mut col_idx = 0;\n103:         let mut start = 0;\n104: \n105: &row_str[start..];\n117:         }\n118: \n119:         \"\"\n120:     }\n121: \n122:     /// Get entire row as iterator over cells\n123:     pub fn row_cells(&self, row: usize) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {\n124:         self.rows\n125:             .get(row)\n126:             .map(|s| s.as_str())\n127:             .unwrap_or(\"\")\n128:             .split(CELL_DELIMITER)\n129:     }\n130: \n131:     /// Set cell value at position\n132:     pub fn set(&mut self, row: usize, col: usize, value: &str) {\n133:         if row >= self.rows.len() {\n134:             return;\n135:         }\n136: \n137:         let cells: Vec<&str> = self.rows[row].split(CELL_DELIMITER).collect();\n138:         let mut new_cells: Vec<String> = cells.iter().map(|s| s.to_string()).collect();\n139: \n140:         while new_cells.len() <= col {\n141:             new_cells.push(String::new());\n142:         }\n143: \n144:         new_cells[col] = value.to_string();\n145:         self.rows[row] = data is empty\n153:     pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n154:         self.rows.is_empty()\n155:     }\n156: }\n157: \n158: /// State for CSV view mode\n159: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n160: pub struct 1) TL;DR
Route mouse and keyboard events through a CSV-aware hit-testing and navigation layer: add a `pixel_to_csv_cell` helper based on `CsvRenderLayout`, introduce `CsvMsg::SelectCell { row, col }`, short‑circuit the text cursor logic in `app.rs` when in CSV view, and have the update loop translate existing cursor movement messages into CSV navigation when CSV mode is active.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### 1. Add `CsvMsg::SelectCell { row, col }`

**a. Extend `CsvMsg`**

In `messages.rs`:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum CsvMsg {
    /// Toggle CSV view mode (parse if entering, discard state if exiting)
    Toggle,
    /// Move cell selection
    MoveUp,
    MoveDown,
    MoveLeft,
    MoveRight,
    /// Move to next cell (Tab)
    NextCell,
    /// Move to previous cell (Shift+Tab)
    PrevCell,
    /// Move to first cell (Cmd+Home)
    FirstCell,
    /// Move to last cell (Cmd+End)
    LastCell,
    /// Move to first column in row (Home)
    RowStart,
    /// Move to last column in row (End)
    RowEnd,
    /// Page up
    PageUp,
    /// Page down
    PageDown,
    /// Exit CSV mode (Escape)
    Exit,
    /// Select a specific cell (mouse hit-test)
    SelectCell { row: usize, col: usize },
}
```

**b. Implement selection handler in `csv.rs`**

In `update_csv`:

```rust
pub fn update_csv(model: &mut AppModel, msg: CsvMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {
    match msg {
        CsvMsg::Toggle => toggle_csv_mode(model),
        CsvMsg::Exit => exit_csv_mode(model),
        CsvMsg::MoveUp => move_selection(model, -1, 0),
        CsvMsg::MoveDown => move_selection(model, 1, 0),
        CsvMsg::MoveLeft => move_selection(model, 0, -1),
        CsvMsg::MoveRight => move_selection(model, 0, 1),
        CsvMsg::NextCell => next_cell(model),
        CsvMsg::PrevCell => prev_cell(model),
        CsvMsg::FirstCell => first_cell(model),
        CsvMsg::LastCell => last_cell(model),
        CsvMsg::RowStart => row_start(model),
        CsvMsg::RowEnd => row_end(model),
        CsvMsg::PageUp => page_up(model),
        CsvMsg::PageDown => page_down(model),
        CsvMsg::SelectCell { row, col } => select_cell(model, row, col),
    }
}
```

Add helper:

    let editor = model.editor_area.focused_editor_mut()?;
    if let Some(csv) = editor.view_mode.as_csv_mut() {
        // Implement a method on CsvState to clamp and adjust viewport
        csv.select_cell(row, col);
        Some(Cmd::Redraw)

    }
}
```

impl CsvState {
    pub fn select_cell(&mut self, row: usize, col: usize) {
        self.selected_cell = CellPosition::new(row, col);
        self.clamp_selection();
        // Ensure it's visible (using CsvViewport API)
        self.viewport.ensure_visible(self.selected_cell);
    }
}

(If `ensure_visible` doesn’t exist yet on `CsvViewport`, add a minimal version that adjusts `top_row`/`left_col` to bring the cell into view.)

---

### 2. Add `pixel_to_csv_cell` hit-testing based on `CsvRenderLayout`

Goal: Use the same layout math as rendering, so hit-testing stays consistent.

**a. Implement a CSV-specific hit-test function (pure)**

Put this in `csv/render.rs` next to `CsvRenderLayout` (keeps CSV geometry together and avoids coupling `view::geometry` to CSV):

```rust
use super::model::{CsvState, CellPosition};
use token::model::editor_area::Rect; // or crate::model::editor_area::Rect depending on path

/// Hit-test a CSV cell given window coordinates.
///
/// Returns None if the click is outside the data grid (e.g., in headers or padding).
pub fn pixel_to_csv_cell(
    csv: &CsvState,
    group_rect: &Rect,
    x: f64,
    y: f64,
    line_height: usize,
    char_width: f32,
    tab_bar_height: usize,
) -> Option<CellPosition> {
    // Convert window coordinates to group-local coordinates
    let local_x = x - group_rect.x as f64;
    let local_y = y - group_rect.y as f64;

    if local_x < 0.0 || local_y < 0.0 {
        return None;
    }

    // Content area starts below the group's tab bar
    let content_y = tab_bar_height; // local Y of content origin
    if local_y < content_y as f64 {
        return None; // in the tab bar, not the CSV content
    }

    // Build the same layout used for rendering.
    // rect_x/rect_w are in *content-local* coordinates.
    let rect_x = 0usize;
    let rect_w = group_rect.width as usize;
    let layout = CsvRenderLayout::calculate(
        csv,
        rect_x,
        rect_w,
        content_y,
        line_height,
        char_width,
    );

    // Ignore clicks in the row header column
    if local_x < (layout.grid_x as f64) {
        return None;
    }

    // Ignore clicks in the column header row for now
    if local_y < layout.data_y as f64 {
        return None;
    }

    // Map Y to row index
    let row_idx_in_view = ((local_y - layout.data_y as f64) / line_height as f64).floor() as usize;
    let row = csv.viewport.top_row + row_idx_in_view;
    if row >= csv.data.row_count() {
        return None;
    }

    // Map X to column index
    let cell_x_in_grid = local_x - layout.grid_x as f64;

    // `visible_columns` are laid out left-to-right with known widths
    for (i, (col_index, col_x_offset)) in layout.visible_columns.iter().enumerate() {
        let col_start = *col_x_offset as f64;
        let col_end = col_start + layout.column_widths_px[i] as f64;
        if cell_x_in_grid >= col_start && cell_x_in_grid < col_end {
            return Some(CellPosition::new(row, *col_index));
        }
    }

    None
}
```

Notes:

- `csv.viewport.top_row` is assumed; adjust the name if it differs.
- This ignores clicks on headers for now; you can extend later to allow “select row/column” behavior.

**b. Expose this via the `Renderer`**

In your `Renderer` implementation (not shown, but you already have `pixel_to_cursor` and `is_in_status_bar` methods), add:

```rust
impl Renderer {
    pub fn pixel_to_csv_cell(&self, x: f64, y: f64, model: &AppModel) -> Option<CellPosition> {
        let group = model.editor_area.focused_group()?;
        let editor = model.editor_area.focused_editor()?;
        let csv = editor.view_mode.as_csv()?;

        csv::render::pixel_to_csv_cell(
            csv,
            &group.rect,
            x,
            y,
            model.line_height,
            self.char_width(),
            model.metrics.tab_bar_height,
        )
    }
}
```

Adjust module paths (`csv::render::pixel_to_csv_cell`) to match your actual hierarchy.

This keeps all csv‑specific math out of `app.rs` and aligns click hit‑testing 1:1 with rendering.

---

### 3. Route mouse clicks through CSV hit-testing in `app.rs`

In `app.rs`, in the `WindowEvent::MouseInput { state: Pressed, button: Left, .. }` arm where you handle left-clicks:

Right now, after some checks, you do:

```rust
let (line, column) = renderer.pixel_to_cursor(x, y, &self.model);
...
// text-mode selection logic
```

Change this block roughly to:

```rust
if let Some(renderer) = &mut self.renderer {
    // Existing status bar / tab bar checks...

    // Focus group logic remains unchanged
    if let Some((group_id, _group)) = self
        .model
        .editor_area
        .group_at_position(x, y)
    {
        if group_id != self.model.editor_area.focused_group_id {
            update(
                &mut self.model,
                Msg::Layout(LayoutMsg::FocusGroup(group_id)),
            );
        }
    }

    // If focused editor is in CSV mode, route click to CSV hit-testing
    let in_csv_mode = {
        let editor = self.model.editor_area.focused_editor();
        editor
            .and_then(|e| Some(e.view_mode.is_csv()))
            .unwrap_or(false)
    };

    if in_csv_mode {
        if let Some(cell) = renderer.pixel_to_csv_cell(x, y, &self.model) {
            // Optionally track click_count for future double-click row/column-selection,
            // but for now a single select is enough.
            return update(
                &mut self.model,
                Msg::Csv(CsvMsg::SelectCell {
                    row: cell.row,
                    col: cell.col,
                }),
            );
        } else {
            // Click outside grid: do nothing or fall back to text behavior if you prefer
            return None;
        }
    }

    // --- existing text-mode click handling continues unchanged ---
    let (line, column) = renderer.pixel_to_cursor(x, y, &self.model);
    ...
}
```

Key points:

- **Short-circuit**: When in CSV mode and the click hits a cell, you must not fall through into `EditorMsg::SetCursorPosition` or selection logic; that’s what currently “eats” input.
- You can still reuse `last_click_time`, `click_count`, etc. for CSV later (e.g., double-click to select row), but it’s not required for basic navigation.

---

### 4. Wire up CSV navigation on keyboard

You want arrows/page up/home/end to move the CSV selection instead of the text cursor when CSV mode is active, **without** needing a new keymap/context right now.

The least invasive solution is to intercept `EditorMsg` movement messages in the central `update` function and forward them to `update_csv` when appropriate.

**a. In `update.rs` (central update function):**

You likely have something like:

```rust
pub fn update(model: &mut AppModel, msg: Msg) -> Option<Cmd> {
    match msg {
        Msg::Editor(editor_msg) => update_editor(model, editor_msg),
        Msg::Document(doc_msg) => update_document(model, doc_msg),
        Msg::Csv(csv_msg) => update_csv(model, csv_msg),
        ...
    }
}
```

Modify the `Msg::Editor` branch or `update_editor` to detect CSV mode:

```rust
match msg {
    Msg::Editor(editor_msg) => {
        if model
            .editor_area
            .focused_editor()
            .map(|e| e.view_mode.is_csv())
            .unwrap_or(false)
        {
            // Map editor movement commands to CSV moves
            if let Some(csv_msg) = map_editor_to_csv(editor_msg) {
                return crate::csv::update_csv(model, csv_msg);
            }
            // For other editor messages (e.g. selection, multi-cursor), you may want to
            // no-op or let them fall through; start by no-op for safety.
            return None;
        }

        update_editor(model, editor_msg)
    }
    ...
}
```

Implement the mapping helper:

```rust
use crate::messages::{EditorMsg, Direction as TextDir, CsvMsg};

fn map_editor_to_csv(editor_msg: EditorMsg) -> Option<CsvMsg> {
    match editor_msg {
        EditorMsg::MoveCursor(TextDir::Up) => Some(CsvMsg::MoveUp),
        EditorMsg::MoveCursor(TextDir::Down) => Some(CsvMsg::MoveDown),
        EditorMsg::MoveCursor(TextDir::Left) => Some(CsvMsg::MoveLeft),
        EditorMsg::MoveCursor(TextDir::Right) => Some(CsvMsg::MoveRight),

        EditorMsg::MoveCursorLineStart => Some(CsvMsg::RowStart),
        EditorMsg::MoveCursorLineEnd => Some(CsvMsg::RowEnd),
        EditorMsg::MoveCursorDocumentStart => Some(CsvMsg::FirstCell),
        EditorMsg::MoveCursorDocumentEnd => Some(CsvMsg::LastCell),

        EditorMsg::PageUp => Some(CsvMsg::PageUp),
        EditorMsg::PageDown => Some(CsvMsg::PageDown),

        // For now, ignore selection variants, word movements, etc.
        _ => None,
    }
}
```

This way:

- Your **existing keymap** stays unchanged; it still emits `EditorMsg` arrows, PageUp, Home, etc.
- When in CSV mode, those messages just **drive CSV navigation** instead of the text cursor.
- When not in CSV mode, behavior is unchanged.

If you already mapped Tab/Shift+Tab to custom commands that emit `CsvMsg::NextCell/PrevCell`, this will coexist; if not, you can later change the keymap without touching runtime/event routing again.

**b. Ensure Escape exits CSV mode**

If you have a command bound to `CsvMsg::Exit`, make sure `update` routes it via the existing `Msg::Csv` branch (`update_csv` already handles `Exit`). If Esc is only bound to editor/selection behavior right now, consider adding a CSV-specific binding in the keymap later; not required for basic navigation.

---

### 5. Rationale and trade-offs

- **Why hit-test in CSV layer, not text?**
  The text hit-testing (`pixel_to_cursor`) works in terms of lines/columns in the document buffer; CSV rendering is a separate layout (headers, row numbers, variable column widths, viewport). Using `CsvRenderLayout` for hit-testing guarantees the grid and click targets stay aligned.
- **Why intercept `EditorMsg` in `update` instead of changing the keymap now?**
  It’s a smaller, localized change: no need to extend `KeyContext`, alter `Command` or rework the default keymap. You get correct arrow/page navigation in CSV mode immediately, with minimal risk of breaking other contexts.
- **Why `CsvMsg::SelectCell`?**
  It keeps CSV selection logic encapsulated in `csv.rs` and `CsvState` (viewport/clamping/selection), and avoids having the runtime modify CSV state directly.

---

### 6. Risks and guardrails

- **Geometry drift**: If rendering layout changes (e.g., padding, header height), `pixel_to_csv_cell` must be updated. Mitigate by:
  - Using `CsvRenderLayout::calculate` for hit-testing (single source of truth).
  - Adding a small unit test that asserts a few known coordinates map to expected cells for a simple CSV (e.g., 3x3).
- **Multi-group/splits**: The implementation assumes hit-testing uses the **focused group**. Ensure you either:
  - Use the group under the mouse for hit-testing (similar to how you focus groups on click), or
  - Always focus the clicked group first (as you already do), then hit-test using `focused_group`.
- **Clicks outside grid**: Currently ignored. If you later want clicks on column headers or row numbers to select entire columns/rows, extend `pixel_to_csv_cell` (or create a more general `CsvHitTestResult` enum).
- **Selection behavior**: Shift+Click, multi-cell selection, etc., are intentionally ignored for CSV now. This keeps the first iteration simple; add them only when needed.

---

### 7. When to consider the advanced path

Consider a more complex design (e.g., keymap/context aware of CSV mode, richer hit-testing) if:

- You want **different keybindings** in CSV mode (e.g., letters editing cells vs. typing into document).
- You introduce **multi-cell selection**, drag-to-select, or column/row selection semantics.
- You allow **in-place editing** of cells with its own text cursor, which will need its own hit-testing and focus management.
- You need **mode-specific commands** exposed to the user (e.g., “sort column”, “filter rows”).

---

### 8. Optional advanced path (brief)

If/when you need a richer CSV UX:

1. **Extend `KeyContext`** with `csv_mode: bool`.
2. **Update keymap** (`load_default_keymap`) to bind arrows, Tab, etc., directly to CSV-specific commands (`Command::CsvMoveUp`, etc.) when `csv_mode` is true.
3. **Make `Command::to_msgs`** emit `Msg::Csv` variants directly, removing the need for interception in `update`.
4. **Generalize hit-testing**: `enum CsvHit { Cell(CellPosition), ColumnHeader(usize), RowHeader(usize), None }` and adjust click behavior accordingly.
5. **Add cell editing mode**: Use a small state machine in `CsvState` to track whether the cell has an active text editor (reusing existing text components for the in-cell editor if possible).

For now, the simple path above should get clicking and arrow-key navigation working reliably with modest changes (scope M: 1–3 hours).

1);\n36:         }\n37: \n38:         // Horizontal scrolling\n39:         if col < self.left_col {\n40:             self.left_col = col;\n41:         } else if col >= self.left_col + self.visible_cols && self.visible_cols > 0 {\n42:             self.left_col = col.saturating_sub(self.visible_cols - 1);\n43:         }\n44: \n45:         // Clamp to valid range\n46:         let max_top = total_rows.saturating_sub(self.visible_rows);\n47:         let max_left = total_cols.saturating_sub(self.visible_cols);\n48:         self.top_row = self.top_row.min(max_top);\n49:         self.left_col = self.left_col.min(max_left);\n50:     }\n51: \n52:     /// Check if a row is visible\n53:     pub fn is_row_visible(&self, row: usize) -> bool {\n54:         row >= self.top_row && row < self.top_row + self.visible_rows\n55:     }\n56: \n57:     /// Check if a column is visible\n58:     pub fn is_col_visible(&self, col: usize) -> bool {\n59:         col >= self.left_col && col < self.left_col + self.visible_cols\n60:     }\n61: }\n62: \n63: #[cfg(test)]\n64: mod tests {\n65:     use super::*;\n66: \n67:     #[test]\n68:     fn test_viewport_ensure_visible_scroll_down() {\n69:         let mut vp = CsvViewport::new(10, 5);\n70:         vp.ensure_visible(15, 0, 100, 20);\n71:         assert_eq!(vp.top_row, 6); // 15 - {
  "diff": "```diff\nIndex: ~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/render.rs\n===================================================================\n--- ~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/render.rs\toriginal\n+++ ~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/render.rs\tmodified\n@@ -117,8 +117,63 @@\n         }\n     }\n }\n \n+/// Hit-test a CSV cell given window coordinates.\n+///\n+/// Returns None if the click is outside the data grid (e.g., in headers or padding).\n+pub fn pixel_to_csv_cell(\n+    csv: &CsvState,\n+    group_rect: &Rect,\n+    x: f64,\n+    y: f64,\n+    line_height: usize,\n+    char_width: f32,\n+    tab_bar_height: usize,\n+) -> Option<CellPosition> {\n+    let local_x = x - group_rect.x as f64;\n+    let local_y = y - group_rect.y as f64;\n+\n+    if local_x < 0.0 || local_y < 0.0 {\n+        return None;\n+    }\n+\n+    let content_y = tab_bar_height;\n+    if local_y < content_y as f64 {\n+        return None;\n+    }\n+\n+    let rect_x = 0usize;\n+    let rect_w = group_rect.width as usize;\n+    let layout = CsvRenderLayout::calculate(csv, rect_x, rect_w, content_y, line_height, char_width);\n+\n+    if local_x < (layout.grid_x as f64) {\n+        return None;\n+    }\n+\n+    if local_y < layout.data_y as f64 {\n+        return None;\n+    }\n+\n+    let row_idx_in_view = ((local_y - layout.data_y as f64) / line_height as f64).floor() as usize;\n+    let row = csv.viewport.top_row + row_idx_in_view;\n+    if row >= csv.data.row_count() {\n+        return None;\n+    }\n+\n+    let cell_x_in_grid = local_x - layout.grid_x as f64;\n+\n+    for (i, (col_index, col_x_offset)) in layout.visible_columns.iter().enumerate() {\n+        let col_start = *col_x_offset as f64;\n+        let col_end = col_start + layout.column_widths_px[i] as f64;\n+        if cell_x_in_grid >= col_start && cell_x_in_grid < col_end {\n+            return Some(CellPosition::new(row, *col_index));\n+        }\n+    }\n+\n+    None\n+}\n+\n #[cfg(test)]\n mod tests {\n     use super::*;\n \n```",

## Assistant

All done! The CSV viewer click/navigation fix is complete. Here's a summary of the changes:

**Changes made:**

1. **[messages.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/messages.rs#L354)** - Added `CsvMsg::SelectCell { row, col }` for mouse click selection

2. **[update/csv.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/csv.rs#L185-L195)** - Added `select_cell` handler

3. **[csv/model.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/model.rs#L219-L229)** - Added `CsvState::select_cell()` method that updates selection and ensures visibility

4. **[csv/render.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/render.rs#L121-L175)** - Added `pixel_to_csv_cell()` hit-testing function using the same `CsvRenderLayout` as rendering

5. **[view/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1485-L1509)** - Added `Renderer::pixel_to_csv_cell()` wrapper method

6. **[runtime/app.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L568-L587)** - Intercepts mouse clicks when in CSV mode and routes to CSV hit-testing

7. **[update/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/mod.rs#L78-L92)** - Added `map_editor_to_csv()` function to translate arrow/page/home/end keys to CSV navigation when in CSV mode

Test with `make csv` - you should now be able to click cells and navigate with arrow keys, Home/End, Page Up/Down.

All fixes are complete. Here's a summary of the changes:

**Scroll wheel support:**
- Added `CsvMsg::ScrollVertical(i32)` and `CsvMsg::ScrollHorizontal(i32)` in [messages.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/messages.rs#L356-L359)
- Added `scroll_vertical()` and `scroll_horizontal()` handlers in [update/csv.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/csv.rs#L199-L219)
- Added the scroll methods to CsvState in [csv/navigation.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/navigation.rs#L127-L145)
- Modified mouse wheel handler in [runtime/app.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L717-L744) to route to CSV mode

**Page up/down already working correctly** - they use `viewport.visible_rows` which is larger than single arrow movements (see lines 93-107 in navigation.rs)

**Selection visibility fix:**
- Restructured rendering in [view/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L821-L913) to:
  1. Draw selection background BEFORE cell text
  2. Draw cell text content
  3. Draw selection border AFTER cell text

This ensures the selected cell's text is visible on top of the highlight background.

Test with `make csv`.

## Assistant

The issue is clear: `viewport.visible_rows` is initialized to 0 (via `Default`) and never gets updated. The rendering code calculates `visible_rows` locally but doesn't update the CsvState.

The fix is to update the viewport size when we calculate it during rendering. Let me add a message to update the viewport size and call it from the render code, OR update it when toggling CSV mode. The simplest approach is to update the CsvState during the toggle based on current window dimensions.

## Assistant

Fixed. The issue was that `viewport.visible_rows` was 0 (default) and never got set, so page up/down only moved 1 row (due to `.max(1)`).

**Changes:**
1. [update/csv.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/csv.rs#L64-L76) - Calculate and set viewport size when entering CSV mode based on window dimensions
2. [update/app.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/app.rs#L20-L33) - Update CSV viewport size on window resize

Now page up/down will jump by the number of visible rows (typically 20-30+ depending on window size). Test with `make csv`.

  "content": "1: # CSV Viewer/Editor Mode\n2: \n3: A spreadsheet-like view for tabular data files (CSV, TSV) with cell editing, horizontal scrolling, and synchronization with the underlying text buffer.\n4: \n5: > **Status:** Phase 1 Complete ✅\n6: > **Inspired by:** [Tablecruncher](https://github.com/Tablecruncher/tablecruncher), TablePlus\n7: > **Target:** ~~Phase 1 (Read-only)~~ → Phase 2 (Cell Editing) → Phase 3 (Advanced Features)\n8: \n9: ---\n10: \n11: ## Design Decisions\n12: \n13: These decisions were resolved during planning:\n14: \n15: | Decision | Resolution |\n16: |----------|------------|\n17: | **State preservation** | Discard state on toggle. Re-parse on each toggle. Simple, stateless approach. |\n18: | **Auto-enable** | No auto-enable. Manual toggle via command palette (\"Toggle CSV View\"). Users can bind their own shortcut. |\n19: | **Size limits** | No hard limits. Implementation should handle large files efficiently. Defer warnings if needed later. |\n20: | **Internal delimiter (0xFA)** | Accepted risk. Re-assess if collisions become an issue in practice. |\n21: \n22: ---\n23: \n24: ## Table of Contents\n25: \n26: 1. [Overview](#1-overview)\n27: 2. [User Experience](#2-user-experience)\n28: 3. [Architecture](#3-architecture)\n29: 4. [Data Model](#4-data-model)\n30: 5. [Parsing Strategy](#5-parsing-strategy)\n31: 6. [Rendering](#6-rendering)\n32: 7. [Cell Navigation & Selection](#7-cell-navigation--selection)\n33: 8. [Cell Editing (Phase 2)](#8-cell-editing-phase-2)\n34: 9. [Synchronization](#9-synchronization)\n35: 10. [Error Handling](#10-error-handling)\n36: 11. [Performance & Benchmarks](#11-performance--benchmarks)\n37: 12. [Testing Strategy](#12-testing-strategy)\n38: 13. [Implementation Phases](#13-implementation-phases)\n39: 14. [Message Types](#14-message-types)\n40: 15. [Commands](#15-commands)\n41: 16. [Configuration](#16-configuration)\n42: \n43: ---\n44: \n45: ## 1. Overview\n46: \n47: ### 1.1 Goals\n48: \n49: - Provide a spreadsheet-like view for CSV/TSV files with rows and columns\n50: - Allow horizontal scrolling when columns exceed viewport width\n51: - Support cell-level editing that syncs back to the underlying text buffer\n52: - Handle large files efficiently (1K, 5K, 50K, 500K rows)\n53: - Graceful error handling for malformed/non-CSV files\n54: \n55: ### 1.2 Non-Goals (Initial Release)\n56: \n57: - Formula evaluation or calculated cells\n58: - Data validation rules\n59: - Import/export to other formats (Excel, JSON)\n60: - Multiple sheets within a single file\n61: - Filtering, sorting, or aggregation\n62: - Column resize with mouse drag (use fixed/auto-width initially)\n63: \n64: ### 1.3 Supported File Types\n65: \n66: | Extension | Delimiter | Detected By |\n67: |-----------|-----------|-------------|\n68: | `.csv`    | `,`       | Extension or content sniffing |\n69: | `.tsv`    | `\\t`      | Extension |\n70: | `.psv`    | `\\|`      | Extension (pipe-separated) |\n71: \n72: ---\n73: \n74: ## 2. User Experience\n75: \n76: ### 2.1 Toggling CSV Mode\n77: \n78: **Entry Point:**\n79: - **Command palette:** \"Toggle CSV View\" command (no default keybinding; users can bind their own)\n80: \n81: **Exit Points:**\n82: 1. Same command toggles off\n83: 2. Press Escape while in CSV mode (returns to text view)\n84: \n85: > **Note:** No auto-enable for `.csv` files. This keeps the behavior explicit and predictable.\n86: \n87: ### 2.2 Visual Layout\n88: \n89: ```\n90: ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n91: │ [file.csv] [×]                                              Tab Bar    │\n92: ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n93: │ ┌─────┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬──────────┐ │\n94: │ │  #  │     A      │     B      │     C      │     D      │    E ... │ │ ◄─ Header Row\n95: │ ├─────┼────────────┼────────────┼────────────┼────────────┼──────────┤ │\n96: │ │  1  │ Alice      │ 30         │ Engineer   │ NYC        │ ...      │ │\n97: │ │  2  │ Bob        │ 25         │ Designer   │ LA         │ ...      │ │\n98: │ │  3  │ Carol      │ 35         │ Manager    │ Chicago    │ ...      │ │\n99: │ │  4  │ ▌█████████ │ (editing)  │            │            │ ...      │ │ ◄─ Active Cell\n100: │ │  5  │ Eve        │ 28         │ DevOps     │ Seattle    │ ...      │ │\n101: │ │ ... │            │            │            │            │          │ │\n102: │ └─────┴────────────┴────────────┴────────────┴────────────┴──────────┘ │\n103: │ ◄────────────────── horizontal scroll ──────────────────────────────► │\n104: ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n105: │ file.csv │ CSV │ Row 4, Col 2 │ 1,234 rows × 12 cols │ (editing)      │ Status Bar\n106: └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n107: ```\n108: \n109: ### 2.3 Navigation\n110: \n111: | Key | Action |\n112: |-----|--------|\n113: | Arrow keys | Move cell selection |\n114: | Tab | Move to next cell (right, then wrap to next row) |\n115: | Shift+Tab | Move to previous cell |\n116: | Enter | Edit current cell / Confirm edit and move down |\n117: | Escape | Cancel edit / Exit CSV mode |\n118: | Cmd+Home | Go to cell A1 |\n119: | Cmd+End | Go to last cell |\n120: | Page Up/Down | Scroll by viewport height |\n121: | Home/End | Go to first/last column in current row |\n122: \n123: ### 2.4 Status Bar Segments\n124: \n125: When CSV mode is active, the status bar shows:\n126: \n127: | Segment | Example | Position |\n128: |---------|---------|----------|\n129: | File name | `data.csv` | Left |\n130: | Mode indicator | `CSV` | Left (new segment) |\n131: | Cell position | `Row 42, Col 3` | Right |\n132: | Dimensions | `1,234 rows × 12 cols` | Right |\n133: | Edit state | `(editing)` | Right (when editing) |\n134: \n135: ---\n136: \n137: ## 3. Architecture\n138: \n139: ### 3.1 Integration with Existing Codebase\n140: \n141: The CSV viewer integrates as an **alternate view mode** for an existing `EditorState`. The same `Document` is shared between text mode and CSV mode.\n142: \n143: ```\n144: ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n145: │                        EditorArea                               │\n146: │  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │\n147: │  │                     EditorState                           │  │\n148: │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │\n149: │  │  │                   ViewMode                          │  │  │\n150: │  │  │  ┌───────────────┐     ┌───────────────────────┐   │  │  │\n151: │  │  │  │   TextMode    │ OR  │      CsvMode          │   │  │  │\n152: │  │  │  │ (default)     │     │ CsvState, CsvViewport │   │  │  │\n153: │  │  │  └───────────────┘     └───────────────────────┘   │  │  │\n154: │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │\n155: │  │                          │                                 │  │\n156: │  │                          ▼                                 │  │\n157: │  │               ┌─────────────────────┐                     │  │\n158: │  │               │     Document        │                     │  │\n159: │  │               │  (Rope text buffer) │                     │  │\n160: │  │               └─────────────────────┘                     │  │\n161: │  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │\n162: └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n163: ```\n164: \n165: ### 3.2 Module Structure\n166: \n167: ```\n168: src/\n169: ├── csv/\n170: │   ├── mod.rs           # Module exports\n171: │   ├── parser.rs        # CSV parsing (using `csv` crate)\n172: │   ├── model.rs         # CsvState, CsvData, Cell types\n173: │   ├── viewport.rs      # CsvViewport calculations\n174: │   ├── navigation.rs    # Cell navigation logic\n175: │   ├── editing.rs       # Cell editing and sync (Phase 2)\n176: │   └── render.rs        # CSV-specific rendering\n177: ├── model/\n178: │   └── editor.rs        # Add ViewMode enum\n179: ├── view/\n180: │   └── mod.rs           # Add CSV rendering dispatch\n181: └── messages.rs          # Add CsvMsg variants\n182: ```\n183: \n184: ### 3.3 Crate Dependencies\n185: \n186: ```toml\n187: [dependencies]\n188: csv = \"1.3\"  # RFC 4180 compliant parser with streaming support\n189: ```\n190: \n191: ### 3.4 Keymap Integration\n192: \n193: CSV mode requires integration with the existing keymap system in `src/keymap/`:\n194: \n195: **Key routing flow:**\n196: 1. Key events captured via `src/runtime/input.rs`\n197: 2. Keymap lookup in `src/keymap/keymap.rs` checks context\n198: 3. CSV mode active → `KeyContext::csv_mode = true`\n199: 4. CSV-specific bindings take precedence when in CSV mode\n200: \n201: **Required additions to `src/keymap/command.rs`:**\n202: ```rust\n203: pub enum KeymapCommand {\n204:     // ... existing commands ...\n205:     \n206:     // CSV mode commands\n207:     CsvToggle,\n208:     CsvMoveUp,\n209:     CsvMoveDown,\n210:     CsvMoveLeft,\n211:     CsvMoveRight,\n212:     CsvNextCell,      // Tab\n213:     CsvPrevCell,      // Shift+Tab\n214:     CsvStartEdit,     // Enter\n215:     CsvConfirmEdit,   // Enter (when editing)\n216:     CsvCancelEdit,    // Escape\n217: }\n218: ```\n219: \n220: **Example keymap.yaml additions:**\n221: ```yaml\n222: - key: shift+cmd+t\n223:   command: CsvToggle\n224:   \n225: - key: tab\n226:   command: CsvNextCell\n227:   when: [csv_mode]\n228:   \n229: - key: shift+tab\n230:   command: CsvPrevCell\n231:   when: [csv_mode]\n232:   \n233: - key: enter\n234:   command: CsvStartEdit\n235:   when: [csv_mode, not_editing]\n236:   \n237: - key: escape\n238:   command: CsvCancelEdit\n239:   when: [csv_mode, editing]\n240: ```\n241: \n242: **Context conditions to add:**\n243: - `csv_mode` - True when editor is in CSV view mode\n244: - `csv_editing` - True when actively editing a cell\n245: \n246: ---\n247: \n248: ## 4. Data Model\n249: \n250: ### 4.1 Core Types\n251: \n252: ```rust\n253: /// View mode for an editor - either text or CSV\n254: /// \n255: /// **State preservation:** State is discarded on toggle (stateless approach).\n256: /// \n257: /// - **Text → CSV:** CsvState freshly initialized, cell (0,0) selected, document re-parsed.\n258: /// - **CSV → Text:** CsvState discarded, text cursor placed at document start.\n259: /// - **Re-enabling CSV:** Full re-parse (no caching).\n260: /// \n261: /// This keeps the implementation simple. Re-parsing is fast (< 100ms for 50K rows).\n262: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n263: pub enum ViewMode {\n264:     #[default]\n265:     Text,\n266:     Csv(CsvState),\n267: }\n268: \n269: /// Complete CSV view state\n270: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n271: pub struct CsvState {\n272:     /// Parsed CSV data (cached)\n273:     pub data: CsvData,\n274:     \n275:     /// Current cell selection (row, column)\n276:     pub selected_cell: CellPosition,\n277:     \n278:     /// Multi-cell selection (optional, for Phase 3)\n279:     pub selection_range: Option<CellRange>,\n280:     \n281:     /// Viewport state for scrolling\n282:     pub viewport: CsvViewport,\n283:     \n284:     /// Whether we're actively editing a cell\n285:     pub editing: Option<CellEditState>,\n286:     \n287:     /// Column widths (computed or configured)\n288:     pub column_widths: Vec<usize>,\n289:     \n290:     /// Whether first row is header\n291:     pub has_header_row: bool,\n292:     \n293:     /// Detected delimiter\n294:     pub delimiter: u8,\n295:     \n296:     /// Parse error (if any)\n297:     pub parse_error: Option<String>,\n298: }\n299: \n300: /// Parsed CSV data\n301: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n302: pub struct CsvData {\n303:     /// All rows as vectors of strings\n304:     /// Using delimited-string storage (Tablecruncher approach) for memory efficiency\n305:     rows: Vec<String>,\n306:     \n307:     /// Field delimiter byte (0xFA for internal storage)\n308:     field_delimiter: u8,\n309:     \n310:     /// Number of columns (max across all rows)\n311:     column_count: usize,\n312:     \n313:     /// Document revision when this was parsed\n314:     parsed_revision: u64,\n315: }\n316: \n317: /// Position in the CSV grid\n318: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]\n319: pub struct CellPosition {\n320:     /// Row index (0-indexed, excluding header if present)\n321:     pub row: usize,\n322:     /// Column index (0-indexed)\n323:     pub col: usize,\n324: }\n325: \n326: /// Range of cells (for future multi-select)\n327: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n328: pub struct CellRange {\n329:     pub start: CellPosition,\n330:     pub end: CellPosition,\n331: }\n332: \n333: /// State for an in-progress cell edit\n334: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n335: pub struct CellEditState {\n336:     /// Position of cell being edited\n337:     pub position: CellPosition,\n338:     /// Current edit buffer\n339:     pub buffer: String,\n340:     /// Cursor position within the buffer\n341:     pub cursor: usize,\n342:     /// Original value (for cancel/undo)\n343:     pub original: String,\n344: }\n345: \n346: /// Viewport for CSV mode (similar to text Viewport but 2D)\n347: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n348: pub struct CsvViewport {\n349:     /// First visible row (0-indexed)\n350:     pub top_row: usize,\n351:     /// First visible column (0-indexed)\n352:     pub left_col: usize,\n353:     /// Number of visible rows\n354:     pub visible_rows: usize,\n355:     /// Number of visible columns (depends on column widths)\n356:     pub visible_cols: usize,\n357: }\n358: ```\n359: \n360: ### 4.2 Memory-Efficient Storage (Tablecruncher Pattern)\n361: \n362: Instead of `Vec<Vec<String>>` (expensive), store each row as a single delimited string:\n363: \n364: ```rust\n365: impl CsvData {\n366:     /// Internal delimiter - uses byte 0xFA\n367:     /// \n368:     /// **Accepted risk:** 0xFA can appear in multi-byte UTF-8 sequences, but this is\n369:     /// rare in typical CSV content. If collision occurs, cell data will be incorrectly\n370:     /// split. Re-assess if this becomes an issue in practice.\n371:     const FIELD_DELIMITER: u8 = 0xFA;\n372:     \n373:     /// Get cell value at (row, col)\n374:     pub fn get(&self, row: usize, col: usize) -> &str {\n375:         if row >= self.rows.len() {\n376:             return \"\";\n377:         }\n378:         self.get_field(&self.rows[row], col)\n379:     }\n380:     \n381:     /// Set cell value at (row, col)\n382:     pub fn set(&mut self, row: usize, col: usize, value: &str) {\n383:         if row >= self.rows.len() {\n384:             return;\n385:         }\n386:         self.rows[row] = self.set_field(&self.rows[row], col, value);\n387:     }\n388:     \n389:     /// Extract field from delimited row string\n390:     fn get_field<'a>(&self, row: &'a str, col: usize) -> &'a str {\n391:         let delim = char::from(Self::FIELD_DELIMITER);\n392:         row.split(delim)\n393:             .nth(col)\n394:             .unwrap_or(\"\")\n395:     }\n396:     \n397:     /// Replace field in delimited row string\n398:     fn set_field(&self, row: &str, col: usize, value: &str) -> String {\n399:         let delim = char::from(Self::FIELD_DELIMITER);\n400:         let mut fields: Vec<&str> = row.split(delim).collect();\n401:         \n402:         // Extend if needed\n403:         while fields.len() <= col {\n404:             fields.push(\"\");\n405:         }\n406:         \n407:         // Replace\n408:         fields[col] = value;\n409:         \n410:         fields.join(&delim.to_string())\n411:     }\n412:     \n413:     /// Row count\n414:     pub fn row_count(&self) -> usize {\n415:         self.rows.len()\n416:     }\n417:     \n418:     /// Column count\n419:     pub fn column_count(&self) -> usize {\n420:         self.column_count\n421:     }\n422: }\n423: ```\n424: \n425: ### 4.3 Column Width Calculation\n426: \n427: ```rust\n428: impl CsvState {\n429:     /// Calculate column widths based on content\n430:     pub fn compute_column_widths(&mut self, max_sample_rows: usize) {\n431:         let sample_count = self.data.row_count().min(max_sample_rows);\n432:         let col_count = self.data.column_count();\n433:         \n434:         // Initialize with header widths or column letters (A, B, C...)\n435:         let mut widths: Vec<usize> = (0..col_count)\n436:             .map(|i| column_letter(i).len().max(3))\n437:             .collect();\n438:         \n439:         // Sample rows to find max width per column\n440:         for row_idx in 0..sample_count {\n441:             for col_idx in 0..col_count {\n442:                 let cell = self.data.get(row_idx, col_idx);\n443:                 let cell_width = cell.chars().count();\n444:                 widths[col_idx] = widths[col_idx].max(cell_width);\n445:             }\n446:         }\n447:         \n448:         // Apply min/max constraints\n449:         const MIN_COL_WIDTH: usize = 4;\n450:         const MAX_COL_WIDTH: usize = 50;\n451:         \n452:         self.column_widths = widths\n453:             .into_iter()\n454:             .map(|w| w.clamp(MIN_COL_WIDTH, MAX_COL_WIDTH))\n455:             .collect();\n456:     }\n457: }\n458: \n459: /// Convert column index to letter (0 = A, 1 = B, ..., 25 = Z, 26 = AA, ...)\n460: /// \n461: /// **Boundary verification required:** The `-1` adjustment at `n / 26 - 1` handles\n462: /// the base-26 bijective numeration correctly. Test cases to verify:\n463: /// - `column_letter(0)` → \"A\"\n464: /// - `column_letter(25)` → \"Z\"  \n465: /// - `column_letter(26)` → \"AA\" (first two-letter)\n466: /// - `column_letter(27)` → \"AB\"\n467: /// - `column_letter(701)` → \"ZZ\" (last two-letter)\n468: /// - `column_letter(702)` → \"AAA\" (first three-letter)\n469: pub fn column_letter(index: usize) -> String {\n470:     let mut result = String::new();\n471:     let mut n = index;\n472:     loop {\n473:         result.insert(0, char::from(b'A' + (n % 26) as u8));\n474:         if n < 26 {\n475:             break;\n476:         }\n477:         n = n / 26 - 1;\n478:     }\n479:     result\n480: }\n481: ```\n482: \n483: ---\n484: \n485: ## 5. Parsing Strategy\n486: \n487: ### 5.1 Initial Parse (Streaming)\n488: \n489: ```rust\n490: use csv::{ReaderBuilder, Terminator};\n491: \n492: /// Parse result with potential warnings\n493: pub struct ParseResult {\n494:     pub data: CsvData,\n495:     pub warnings: Vec<ParseWarning>,\n496: }\n497: \n498: pub enum ParseWarning {\n499:     /// Rows have different column counts\n500:     InconsistentColumns { expected: usize, found: usize, row: usize },\n[... omitted lines 501 to 1747 ...]\n1748:         assert_eq!(result.data.get(0, 0), \"a\"); // BOM should be stripped\n1749:     }\n1750:     \n1751:     #[test]\n1752:     fn test_empty_file() {\n1753:         let csv = \"\";\n1754:         let result = parse_csv(csv, b',', None).unwrap();\n1755:         assert_eq!(result.data.row_count(), 0);\n1756:         assert_eq!(result.data.column_count(), 0);\n1757:     }\n1758:     \n1759:     #[test]\n1760:     fn test_single_column_csv() {\n1761:         let csv = \"a\\nb\\nc\";\n1762:         let result = parse_csv(csv, b',', None).unwrap();\n1763:         assert_eq!(result.data.column_count(), 1);\n1764:         assert_eq!(result.data.row_count(), 3);\n1765:     }\n1766:     \n1767:     #[test]\n1768:     fn test_trailing_delimiter() {\n1769:         let csv = \"a,b,c,\\n1,2,3,\";\n1770:         let result = parse_csv(csv, b',', None).unwrap();\n1771:         assert_eq!(result.data.column_count(), 4); // Empty trailing field\n1772:     }\n1773:     \n1774:     #[test]\n1775:     fn test_unicode_content() {\n1776:         let csv = \"名前,説明\\nアリス,こんにちは\\n🎉,emoji\";\n1777:         let result = parse_csv(csv, b',', None).unwrap();\n1778:         assert_eq!(result.data.get(0, 0), \"名前\");\n1779:         assert_eq!(result.data.get(2, 0), \"🎉\");\n1780:     }\n1781: }\n1782: ```\n1783: \n1784: ### 12.2 Integration Tests\n1785: \n1786: ```rust\n1787: // tests/csv_integration.rs\n1788: \n1789: #[test]\n1790: fn test_toggle_csv_mode() {\n1791:     let mut model = create_test_model_with_file(\"test.csv\", \"a,b,c\\n1,2,3\");\n1792:     \n1793:     // Toggle on\n1794:    \n1812:     // Undo\n1813:     model.handle_message(Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::Undo));\n1814:     \n1815:     // CSV should be re-synced\n1816:     if let ViewMode::Csv(csv) = model.status_bar();\n1870:     assert!(status.contains_segment(\"CSV\"));\n1871:     assert!(status.contains_segment(\"Row 1, Col 1\")); // or similar\n1872: }\n1873: ```\n1874: \n1875: ### 12.3 Stress Tests\n1876: \n1877: ```rust\n1878: // tests/csv_stress.rs\n1879: \n1880: #[test]\n1881: fn test_large_csv_1k() {\n1882:     let csv = generate_csv(1_000, 20);\n1883:     let start = std::time::Instant::now();\n1884:     let result = parse_csv(&csv, b',', None).unwrap();\n1885:     let duration = start.elapsed();\n1886:     \n1887:     assert_eq!(result.data.row_count(), 1001); // ```rust\n1948: // tests/csv_render.rs\n1949: \n1950: #[test]\n1951: fn test_csv_grid_render_empty() {\n1952:     let mut model = create_test_model_with_file(\"test.csv\", \"\");\n1953: should have selection border\n1976:     // (This would require render inspection or snapshot testing)\n1977: }\n1978: ```\n1979: \n1980: ---\n1981: \n1982: ## 13. Implementation Phases\n1983: \n1984: ### Phase 1: Read-Only Viewer (MVP)\n1985: \n1986: **Goals:** Display CSV as grid, navigate cells, horizontal scrolling\n1987: \n1988: **Deliverables:**\n1989: - [ ] `CsvState` and `CsvData` types\n1990: - [ ] CSV parsing with `csv` crate\n1991: - [ ] Delimiter detection\n1992: - [ ] Grid rendering (cells, headers, row numbers)\n1993: - [ ] Cell navigation (arrow keys, Tab)\n1994: - [ ] Horizontal scroll for many columns\n1995: - [ ] Toggle command (`Cmd+Shift+T`)\n1996: - [ ] Status bar integration\n1997: - [ ] Basic error handling (show in status bar)\n1998: - [ ] Unit tests for parsing\n1999: \n2000: **Estimated effort:** 3-4 days\n2001: \n2002: ### Phase 2: Cell Editing\n2003: \n2004: **Goals:** Edit cells in place, sync to document buffer\n2005: \n2006: **Deliverables:**\n2007: - [ ] `CellEditState` and edit lifecycle\n2008: - [ ] Cell editor rendering (cursor, input)\n2009: - [ ] Keyboard input while editing\n2010: - [ ] Confirm (Enter) / Cancel (Escape)\n2011: - [ ] Sync cell edit → document buffer\n2012: - [ ] Sync document changes → CSV (re-parse)\n2013: - [ ] Undo/redo integration\n2014: - [ ] Integration tests for edit sync\n2015: \n2016: **Estimated effort:** 2-3 days\n2017: \n2018: ### Phase 3: Polish & Performance\n2019: \n2020: **Goals:** Large file support, UX improvements\n2021: \n2022: **Deliverables:**\n2023: - [ ] Performance benchmarks\n2024: - [ ] Virtual scrolling optimization\n2025: - [ ] Progress indicator for large files\n2026: - [ ] Column auto-width improvements\n2027: - [ ] Mouse click to select cell\n2028: - [ ] Copy cell value (Cmd+C)\n2029: - [ ] Stress tests (500K rows)\n2030: \n2031: **Estimated effort:** 2-3 days\n2032: \n2033: ### Phase 4: Advanced Features (Future)\n2034: \n2035: **Potential features:**\n2036: - [ ] Multi-cell selection (Shift+Arrow, Shift+Click)\n2037: - [ ] Copy/paste range\n2038: - [ ] Column resize (mouse drag)\n2039: - [ ] Sort by column (click header)\n2040: - [ ] Filter rows\n2041: - [ ] Find in CSV\n2042: - [ ] Import/export formats\n2043: \n2044: ---\n2045: \n2046: ## 14. Message Types\n2047: \n2048: ```rust\n2049: /// CSV-specific messages\n2050: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n2051: pub enum CsvMsg {\n2052:     /// Toggle CSV mode on/off\n2053:     ToggleCsvMode,\n2054:     \n2055:     /// Force enable CSV mode (even if detection fails)\n2056:     EnableCsvMode { delimiter: Option<u8> },\n2057:     \n2058:     /// Disable CSV mode\n2059:     DisableCsvMode,\n2060:     \n2061:     // === Navigation ===\n2062:     /// Move cell selection\n2063:     MoveSelection { delta_row: i32, delta_col: i32 },\n2064:     /// Tab to next cell\n2065:     NextCell,\n2066:     /// Shift+Tab to previous cell\n2067:     PrevCell,\n2068:     /// Go to specific cell\n2069:     GoToCell { row: usize, col: usize },\n2070:     /// Go to first cell (Cmd+Home)\n2071:     GoToFirstCell,\n2072:     /// Go to last cell (Cmd+End)\n2073:     GoToLastCell,\n2074:     \n2075:     // === Scrolling ===\n2076:     /// Scroll horizontally by columns\n2077:     ScrollHorizontal(i32),\n2078:     /// Page up\n2079:     PageUp,\n2080:     /// Page down\n2081:     PageDown,\n2082:     \n2083:     // === Editing (Phase 2) ===\n2084:     /// Start editing selected cell\n2085:     StartEditing,\n2086:     /// Start editing with initial character\n2087:     StartEditingWithChar(char),\n2088:     /// Confirm edit and update data\n2089:     ConfirmEdit,\n2090:     /// Cancel edit\n2091:     CancelEdit,\n2092:     /// Insert character while editing\n2093:     EditInsertChar(char),\n2094:     /// Delete backward while editing\n2095:     EditDeleteBackward,\n2096:     /// Move cursor left in edit buffer\n2097:     EditCursorLeft,\n2098:     /// Move cursor right in edit buffer\n2099:     EditCursorRight,\n2100:     \n2101:     // === Mouse ===\n2102:     /// Click on cell\n2103:     ClickCell { row: usize, col: usize },\n2104: }\n2105: ```\n2106: \n2107: ---\n2108: \n2109: ## 15. Commands\n2110: \n2111: Add to `src/commands.rs`:\n2112: \n2113: ```rust\n2114: pub enum CommandId {\n2115:     // ... existing commands ...\n2116:     \n2117:     /// Toggle CSV viewer mode\n2118:     ToggleCsvView,\n2119: }\n2120: \n2121: pub static COMMANDS: &[CommandDef] = &[\n2122:     // ... existing commands ...\n2123:     \n2124:     CommandDef {\n2125:         id: CommandId::ToggleCsvView,\n2126:         label: \"Toggle CSV View\",\n2127:         keybinding: Some(\"⇧⌘T\"),\n2128:     },\n2129: ];\n2130: ```\n2131: \n2132: ---\n2133: \n2134: ## 16. Configuration\n2135: \n2136: ### 16.1 Theme Extensions\n2137: \n2138: Add to `theme.rs`:\n2139: \n2140: ```rust\n2141: pub struct CsvTheme {\n2142:     /// Header row background\n2143:     pub header_background: Color,\n2144:     /// Header row foreground\n2145:     pub header_foreground: Color,\n2146:     /// Row number (gutter) background\n2147:     pub row_number_background: Color,\n2148:     /// Row number foreground\n2149:     pub row_number_foreground: Color,\n2150:     /// Grid line color\n2151:     pub grid_line: Color,\n2152:     /// Selected cell border color\n2153:     pub selection_border: Color,\n2154:     /// Number cell foreground (right-aligned)\n2155:     pub number_foreground: Color,\n2156:     /// Alternating row background (optional)\n2157:     pub alternate_row_background: Option<Color>,\n2158: }\n2159: \n2160: /// Default fallbacks when theme has no `csv` section\n2161: /// (Current themes: dark, fleet-dark, github-dark, github-light lack csv sections)\n2162: impl Default for CsvTheme {\n2163:     fn default() -> Self {\n2164:         // Derive from existing theme fields\n2165:         Self {\n2166:             header_background: theme.gutter.background,\n2167:             header_foreground: theme.gutter.foreground_active,\n2168:             row_number_background: theme.gutter.background,\n2169:             row_number_foreground: theme.gutter.foreground,\n2170:             grid_line: theme.gutter.border_color,\n2171:             selection_border: theme.editor.cursor_color,\n2172:             number_foreground: theme.syntax.number,\n2173:             alternate_row_background: None,\n2174:         }\n2175:     }\n2176: }\n2177: ```\n2178: \n2179: ### 16.2 Editor Config Extensions\n2180: \n2181: Add to `config.rs`:\n2182: \n2183: ```rust\n2184: pub struct CsvConfig {\n2185:     /// Treat first row as header\n2186:     pub default_has_header: bool,\n2187:     /// Maximum rows to sample for column width calculation\n2188:     pub width_sample_rows: usize,\n2189:     /// Minimum column width (characters)\n2190:     pub min_column_width: usize,\n2191:     /// Maximum column width (characters)\n2192:     pub max_column_width: usize,\n2193: }\n2194: \n2195: impl Default for CsvConfig {\n2196:     fn default() -> Self {\n2197:         Self {\n2198:             default_has_header: true,\n2199:             width_sample_rows: 1000,\n2200:             min_column_width: 4,\n2201:             max_column_width: 50,\n2202:         }\n2203:     }\n2204: }\n2205: ```\n2206: \n2207: > **Note:** No `auto_enable` setting—CSV mode is always manual via command palette.\n2208: > No size limits are enforced; large files should work with the virtual scrolling implementation.\n2209: LanguageId::from_path(&path);\n2229:     \n2230:     // Could add CSV auto-detection here if language.is_csv_like()\n2231:     \n2232:     Ok(Self { /* ... */ })\n2233: }\n2234: ```\n2235: \n2236:   "content": "1: # Changelog\n2: \n3: All notable changes to rust-editor are documented in this file.\n4: \n5: ---\n6: \n7: ## v0.3.4 - 2025-12-16 (Latest)\n8: \n9: ### Fixed - HiDPI Display Switching\n10: \n11: Fixed critical issues when switching between displays with different DPI/scale factors (e.g., moving window between Retina and non-Retina monitors):\n12: \n13: - **Surface resize on display change** - The softbuffer Surface is now explicitly resized after creation, fixing incorrect rendering when switching displays\n14: - **Buffer bounds checking in Frame** - Frame::new now validates buffer size matches dimensions, preventing panics during display transitions\n15: - **Dynamic tab bar height** - Tab bar height is now computed from actual glyph metrics (`line_height + padding * 2`) instead of hardcoded values\n16: - **Scaled metrics throughout** - Model's `resize()` and `set_char_width()` now use properly scaled metrics for viewport calculations\n17: \n18: ### Changed\n19: \n20: - `ScaleFactorChanged` now triggers both `ReinitializeRenderer` and `Redraw` commands to ensure immediate visual update\n21: - `reinit_renderer` recomputes tab bar height and viewport geometry after font metrics change\n22: - Viewport visible lines calculation now accounts for tab bar height\n23: \n24: ### Technical Details\n25: \n26: - `Renderer::with_scale_factor` explicitly calls `surface.resize()` after creation\n27: - `Frame::new` adjusts height if buffer is smaller than expected (`width * height`)\n28: - `recompute_tab_bar_height_from_line_height()` added to AppModel for font-metric-based sizing\n29: - `group_content_rect_scaled()` now used in rendering for DPI-aware content areas\n30: \n31: ---\n32: \n33: ## v0.3.3 - 2025-12-15\n34: \n35: ### Added - Phase 3-5 Languages for Syntax Highlighting\n36: \n37: Added 11 new languages to syntax highlighting, completing the roadmap phases 3-5:\n38: \n39: **Phase 3 (Priority):**\n40: - **TypeScript** - `tree-sitter-typescript` v0.23 with custom queries\n41: - **TSX** - Shares queries with TypeScript, separate parser\n42: - **JSON** - `tree-sitter-json` v0.24 with custom queries\n43: - **TOML** - `tree-sitter-toml-ng` v0.7 with custom queries\n44: \n45: **Phase 4 (Common):**\n46: - **Python** - `tree-sitter-python` v0.25 using built-in highlights query\n47: - **Go** - `tree-sitter-go` v0.25 using built-in highlights query\n48: - **PHP** - `tree-sitter-php` v0.24 using built-in highlights query\n49: \n50: **Phase 5 (Extended):**\n51: - **C** - `tree-sitter-c` v0.24 using built-in highlights query\n52: - **C++** - `tree-sitter-cpp` v0.23 using built-in highlights query\n53: - **Java** - `tree-sitter-java` v0.23 using built-in highlights query\n54: - **Bash** - `tree-sitter-bash` v0.25 using built-in highlights query\n55: \n56: **Total languages now supported: 17** (PlainText excluded)\n57: \n58: ### Changed\n59: \n60: - Upgraded `tree-sitter` from 0.24 to 0.25 for ABI compatibility with newer grammars\n61: - `LanguageId` enum now includes all 17 language variants\n62: - `from_extension()` recognizes extended file extensions (.tsx, .mts, .cts, .pyw, etc.)\n63: - `from_path()` recognizes special filenames (Makefile, Dockerfile, .bashrc, etc.)\n64: \n65: ### Technical Details\n66: \n67: - Custom queries created for TypeScript, JSON, TOML (in `queries/` directory)\n68: - Phase 4-5 languages use built-in `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` or `HIGHLIGHT_QUERY` constants\n69: - Query compilation tests added for all 17 languages\n70: - Parsing tests added for all new languages\n71: - 671 total tests passing\n72: \n73: ---\n74: \n75: ## v0.3.2 - 2025-12-15\n76: \n77: ### Added - Incremental Parsing with tree.edit()\n78: \n79: Implemented proper incremental parsing for significantly faster syntax highlighting on edits:\n80: \n81: **Tree Caching:**\n82: - `ParserState` now caches parsed trees per document in `doc_cache: HashMap<DocumentId, DocParseState>`\n83: - Each `DocParseState` stores the tree, source text, and language\n84: - Cache enables incremental parsing on subsequent edits\n85: \n86: **Edit Diffing:**\n87: - `compute_incremental_edit()` computes `InputEdit` by diffing old vs new source\n88: - Finds common prefix/suffix to minimize the edited region\n89: - `byte_to_point()` converts byte offsets to tree-sitter `Point` (row, column)\n90: \n91: **Incremental Parse Flow:**\n92: 1. On edit, diff cached source against new source\n93: 2. Call `tree.edit(&input_edit)` on cached tree\n94: 3. Pass edited tree to `parser.parse(source, Some(&old_tree))`\n95: 4. Tree-sitter reuses unchanged nodes, only re-parsing the changed region\n96: \n97: **Performance Results:**\n98: | File Size | Full Reparse | Incremental |\n99: |-----------|--------------|-------------|\n100: | 100 lines | 67µs | 68µs |\n101: | 500 lines | 330µs | 356µs |\n102: | 1000 lines | 660µs | 728µs |\n103: | 5000 lines | 3.4ms | 3.6ms |\n104: \n105: *Note: Incremental is similar speed due to highlight extraction dominating; benefit increases for larger files.*\n106: \n107: **New Benchmark Suite** (`benches/syntax.rs`):\n108: - `parse_sample` - Parse small samples for each language\n109: - `parse_only_sample` - Isolated parse time (pre-initialized parser)\n110: - `parse_only_large_rust` - Large file scaling (100-10000 lines)\n111: - `incremental_parse_small_edit` - Incremental with append\n112: - `incremental_parse_middle_edit` - Incremental with mid-file edit\n113: - `full_reparse_comparison` - Fresh parser baseline\n114: \n115: **New Makefile Target:**\n116: - `make bench-syntax` - Run syntax highlighting benchmarks\n117: \n118: ### Changed\n119: \n120: - `ParserState` now includes `doc_cache` for tree caching\n121: - `parse_and_highlight()` uses cached trees for incremental parsing\n122: - Added `clear_doc_cache()` method for document cleanup\n123: \n124: ---\n125: \n126: ## v0.3.1 - 2025-12-15\n127: \n128: ### Fixed - Syntax Highlighting Bugs\n129: \n130: Critical fixes for the syntax highlighting system:\n131: \n132: **Tree-sitter Incremental Parsing Bug:**\n133: - Fixed misaligned highlights after document edits (e.g., pressing Enter)\n134: - Root cause: Passing old cached tree to tree-sitter without calling `tree.edit()`\n135: - Tree-sitter incorrectly reused nodes from stale tree, producing wrong line/column positions\n136: - Fix: Always do full reparse by passing `None` instead of cached tree\n137: - Removed unused tree caching from `ParserState` until proper incremental parsing is implemented\n138: \n139: **Flash of Unstyled Text (FOUC):**\n140: - Fixed jarring unstyled flash during syntax re-parsing\n141: - Old highlights are now preserved until new ones arrive\n142: - Revision checks ensure only matching highlights are applied\n143: - Reduced debounce from 50ms to 30ms for snappier updates\n144: \n145: **Tab Expansion in Highlighting:**\n146: - Fixed highlight token columns not accounting for tab expansion\n147: - Token character columns are now converted to visual columns using `char_col_to_visual_col()`\n148: \n149: ### Changed\n150: \n151: - `SYNTAX_DEBOUNCE_MS` reduced from 50ms to 30ms\n152: - `ParserState` no longer caches syntax trees (simplified until incremental parsing)\n153: \n154: ---\n155: \n156: ## v0.3.0 - 2025-12-15\n157: \n158: ### Added -
