  "content": "1: # Token - A Multi-Cursor Text Editor\n2: \n3: **Multi-cursor, code editor inspired by JetBrains IDEs, Vibe-coded in Rust, using Amp Code.**\n4: <br>\n5: Most(ish) of the threads, prompts and conversations with the agent is available to view on\n6: my [Amp profile](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre).\n7: \n8: ![Rust](https://img.shields.io/badge/Rust-000000.svg?logo=rust&logoColor=white&style=flat)\n9: [![Amp](https://img.shields.io/badge/Amp%20Code-191C19.svg?logo=data:image/svg%2bxml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMjEiIGhlaWdodD0iMjEiIHZpZXdCb3g9IjAgMCAyMSAyMSIgZmlsbD0ibm9uZSIgeG1sbnM9Imh0dHA6Ly93d3cudzMub3JnLzIwMDAvc3ZnIj4KPHBhdGggZD0iTTMuNzY4NzkgMTguMzAxNUw4LjQ5ODM5IDEzLjUwNUwxMC4yMTk2IDIwLjAzOTlMMTIuNzIgMTkuMzU2MUwxMC4yMjg4IDkuODY3NDlMMC44OTA4NzYgNy4zMzg0NEwwLjIyNTk0IDkuODkzMzFMNi42NTEzNCAxMS42Mzg4TDEuOTQxMzggMTYuNDI4MkwzLjc2ODc5IDE4LjMwMTVaIiBmaWxsPSIjRjM0RTNGIi8+CjxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0xNy40MDc0IDEyLjc0MTRMMTkuOTA3OCAxMi4wNTc1TDE3LjQxNjcgMi41Njg5N0w4LjA3ODczIDAuMDM5OTI0Nkw3LjQxMzggMi41OTQ4TDE1LjI5OTIgNC43MzY4NUwxNy40MDc0IDEyLjc0MTRaIiBmaWxsPSIjRjM0RTNGIi8+CjxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0xMy44MTg0IDE2LjM4ODNMMTYuMzE4OCAxNS43MDQ0TDEzLjgyNzYgNi4yMTU4OEw0LjQ4OTcxIDMuNjg2ODNMMy44MjQ3NyA2LjI0MTcxTDExLjcxMDEgOC4zODM3NkwxMy44MTg0IDE2LjM4ODNaIiBmaWxsPSIjRjM0RTNGIi8+Cjwvc3ZnPg==&style=flat)](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre)\n10: ![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-007ACC.svg?style=flat)\n11: \n12: <img src=\"assets/screenshot.png\" alt=\"Token Screenshot\" width=\"868\"/>\n13: \n14: ---\n15: \n16: ## Building from source\n17: \n18: To build Token from source, you will need the [Rust toolchain](https://rustup.rs/) installed.\n19: The `makefile setup` takes care of installing any other dependencies.\n20: \n21: ```shell\n22: git clone https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token\n23: cd token\n24: \n25: # Install dependencies (and misc tools)\n26: make setup\n27: \n28: # Build and run with Makefile\n29: make dev\n30: \n31: # Or run seperately\n32: make build\n33: make run\n34: \n35: # Or build and run manually with cargo,\n36: # like a regular boring person...\n37: cargo build --release\n38: cargo run\n39: ```\n40: \n41: ## Commands\n42: \n43: List them with `make help`\n44: \n45: ### Build & Run\n46: \n47: | Command        | Description                                      |\n48: | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |\n49: | `make build`   | Build debug binary                               |\n50: | `make release` | Build optimized release binary                   |\n51: | `make run`     | Run with default sample file (indentation.txt)   |\n52: | `make dev`     | Run debug build (faster compile, slower runtime) |\n53: | `make clean`   | Remove build artifacts                           |\n54: | `make fmt`     | Format Rust code and markdown files              |\n55: \n56: ### Testing\n57: \n58: | Command             | Description                            |\n59: | ------------------- | -------------------------------------- |\n60: | `make test`         | Run all tests                          |\n61: | `make test-verbose` | Run tests with output                  |\n62: | `make test-fast`    | Run tests faster using `cargo nextest` |\n63: \n64: ---\n65: \n66: ## The Hidden Complexity of Text Editors\n67: \n68: You use a text editor every day. But do you actually understand what it's doing?\n69: \n70: On the surface, an editor is \"just\" text on a screen with a blinking cursor. Underneath, it's a small physics engine for\n71: glyphs, pixels, and input events—quietly solving dozens of problems every time you press a key.\n72: \n73: Consider a few things you rely on constantly but probably don't think about:\n74: \n75: - **Cursor + selection choreography** — Hold Shift and press arrow keys: the cursor moves, the selection grows or\n76:   shrinks, and the viewport sometimes nudges just enough to keep everything visible. Double-click a word, then\n77:   Shift+Click somewhere else. The rules feel obvious—until you try to implement them.\n78: \n79: - **Keyboard modifier edge cases** — What's the \"right\" behavior for Ctrl+Left on a line with emoji, snake_case, and\n80:   camelCase? What happens to your selection when you hit Cmd+Z, then Cmd+Shift+Z, then type? When should Alt extend\n81:   selection, Cmd jump by word, or Option+Option+Arrow add a new cursor?\n82: \n83: - **Glyphs that don't behave like characters** — A single \"character\" on screen might be multiple codepoints: combining\n84:   accents, zero-width joiners, ligatures. The cursor should snap to visually sensible positions, not halfway through a\n85:   grapheme cluster. Column count, byte offset, and rendered width all disagree—and the editor must reconcile them.\n86: \n87: - **Viewport scrolling that feels \"natural\"** — Page Down doesn't simply add `viewportHeight` to `scrollY`. It decides\n88:   which line becomes the new top, keeps the cursor in a \"safe zone,\" and handles partial lines at edges. Mouse wheel,\n89:   scrollbar drag, and \"Go to Line\" all produce subtly different scroll behavior—but never feel inconsistent when done\n90:   right.\n91: \n92: All of this hides behind a UI that's supposed to feel invisible. That's exactly why it's easy to underestimate the\n93: complexity.\n94: \n95: **Why this matters for AI-assisted development:** If you want AI agents to help design, debug, or extend an editor, they\n96: need more than \"there's a cursor and some lines.\" They need a precise, shared vocabulary for scroll offsets, viewports,\n97: cursor modes, selection semantics, and edge cases. The same is true for humans: explicit documentation is how you\n98: sanity-check your own assumptions and avoid rediscovering tricky behaviors through trial and error.\n99: \n100: Token is built around that idea: make geometry, behavior, and edge cases **fully explicit**—for both humans and\n101: machines. See [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) for our attempt to turn \"obvious\" editor behavior\n102: into a concrete, testable spec.\n103: \n104: ---\n105: \n106: ## AI-Assisted Development: A Framework for Complex Projects\n107: \n108: Token was built primarily through conversations with [Amp Code](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre). This section\n109: documents the methodology that made it work—applicable to any non-trivial project where you're collaborating with AI\n110: agents.\n111: \n112: ### The Core Principle\n113: \n114: AI agents excel at focused, well-defined tasks. The human's job is to provide **structure**: clear phases, written\n115: specifications, and explicit invariants. The more complex your project, the more this structure pays off.\n116: \n117: ---\n118: \n119: ### Three Modes of Work\n120: \n121: Before each session, explicitly state which mode you're in:\n122: \n123: | Mode        | Purpose                                       | Inputs                       | Example                          |\n124: | ----------- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------- |\n125: | **Build**   | New behavior that didn't exist                | Feature spec, reference docs | \"Implement split view (Phase 3)\" |\n126: | **Improve** | Better architecture without changing behavior | Organization docs, roadmap   | \"Extract modules from main.rs\"   |\n127: | **Sweep**   | Fix a cluster of related bugs                 | Bug tracker, gap doc         | \"Multi-cursor selection bugs\"    |\n128: \n129: This prevents scope creep and keeps AI contributions coherent across sessions.\n130: \n131: ---\n132: \n133: ### Design Before Code\n134: \n135: For complex features, invest in upfront documentation:\n136: \n137: **1. Reference Documentation**  \n138: Create a \"document of truth\" for cross-cutting concerns. [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) defines\n139: viewport math, coordinate systems, and scrolling behavior—used across split view, selection, and overlay\n140: implementations.\n141: \n142: **2. Feature Specifications**  \n143: Before implementing multi-cursor, we wrote [SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md](docs/archived/SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md):\n144: \n145: - Data structures and invariants\n146: - Keyboard shortcuts table\n147: - Message enums and expected behavior\n148: - Multi-phase implementation plan\n149: \n150: **3. Gap Documents**  \n151: When a feature is 60-90% complete, create a gap\n152: doc. [MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md](docs/archived/MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md) lists:\n153: \n154: - What's implemented vs. missing\n155: - Design decisions for each gap\n156: - Tests and success criteria\n157: \n158: This turns \"vague incompleteness\" into concrete, actionable tasks.\n159: \n160: ---\n161: \n162: ### The Multi-Cursor Migration: A Case Study\n163: \n164: Adding multi-cursor to a single-cursor editor touched nearly every file. Here's how we avoided chaos:\n165: \n166: **1. Wrote invariants upfront:**\n167: \n168: ```rust\n169: // MUST maintain: cursors.len() == selections.len()\n170: // MUST maintain: cursors[i].to_position() == selections[i].head\n171: ```\n172: \n173: **2. Created migration helpers:**\n174: \n175: ```rust\n176: // Old code still works via accessor\n177: impl AppModel {\n178:     pub fn cursor(&self) -> &Cursor { &self.editor.cursors[0] }\n179: }\n180: ```\n181: \n182: **3. Implemented in phases:**\n183: \n184: - Phase 0: Per-cursor primitives (`move_cursor_left_at(idx)`)\n185: - Phase 1: All-cursor wrappers (`move_all_cursors_left()`)\n186: - Phase 2-4: Update handlers, add tests\n187: - Phase 5: Bug sweep for edge cases\n188: \n189: **4. Ran targeted sweeps:**\n190: When bugs emerged, we created a focused tracker and fixed them systematically—not one-off. This leverages AI's strength\n191: at \"apply this pattern everywhere.\"\n192: \n193: ---\n194: \n195: ### Agent Configuration\n196: \n197: Tell agents how to work in your codebase:\n198: \n199: | File        | Purpose                                         |\n200: | ----------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n201: | `AGENTS.md` | Build commands, architecture, conventions       |\n202: | `CLAUDE.md` | Same (symlink or duplicate for different tools) |\n203: \n204: Key: specify your Makefile/scripts so agents use `make test` instead of inventing\n205: `cargo test --all-features --no-fail-fast`.\n206: \n207: ---\n208: \n209: ### Documentation Structure\n210: \n211: | Path                                                       | Purpose                                             |\n212: | ---------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |\n213: | [docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md)                         | Planned features with design doc links              |\n214: | [docs/CHANGELOG.md](docs/CHANGELOG.md)                     | Completed work (reference when things break)        |\n215: | [docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) | Domain reference (geometry, coordinates, scrolling) |\n216: | `docs/feature/`                                            | Design specs for planned features                   |\n217: | `docs/archived/`                                           | Completed feature specs (kept for reference)        |\n218: \n219: ---\n220: \n221: ### Agent Workflow Patterns\n222: \n223: **Research → Synthesize → Implement:**\n224: \n225: 1. Use **Librarian** to research how VSCode/Zed/Helix solve a problem\n226: 2. Use **Oracle** to review findings and produce a design doc\n227: 3. Implement in phases with tests\n228: \n229: **Review Before Implementation:**\n230: Having Oracle review [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) caught 15+ issues (off-by-one errors,\n231: division-by-zero edge cases) before they became bugs in code.\n232: \n233: ---\n234: \n235: ## Development Timeline\n236: \n237: Token's development followed distinct phases, each with focused objectives:\n238: \n239: | Phase               | Dates          | Focus                                           |\n240: | ------------------- | -------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n241: | Foundation          | Sep 26 - Dec 5 | Setup, reference docs, architecture             |\n242: | Research Sprint     | Dec 6          | Performance, keymaps, testing infrastructure    |\n243: | Feature Development | Dec 5-6        | Split view, undo/redo, multi-cursor selection   |\n244: | Codebase Refactor   | Dec 6          | Extract modules from main.rs (3100→20 lines)    |\n245: | Research & Polish   | Dec 7          | Zed research, cursor API fixes, test extraction |\n246: | Maintenance         | Dec 7          | Bugfixes, benchmarks, documentation             |\n247: \n248: ---\n249: \n250: ### Notable Threads\n251: \n252: <details>\n253: <summary><strong>🔮 Oracle: UI Reference Deep Review</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-7b92a860-a2f7-4397-985c-73b2fa3e9582\">T-7b92a860</a></summary>\n254: \n255: **Date**: 2025-12-03\n256: \n257: The Oracle performed a comprehensive technical review of EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md and identified **15+ issues**:\n258: \n259: **Critical Bugs Found**:\n260: \n261: - Off-by-one error in viewport calculations: `lastVisibleLine` should be `firstVisibleLine + visibleLines - 1`\n262: - Division-by-zero edge cases in scrollbar thumb calculations\n263: - `preferredColumn` was documented as column index but code used pixel X values\n264: \n265: **Semantic Issues**:\n266: \n267: - Selection struct uses `anchor/head` internally but docs said `start/end`\n268: - Missing coverage for folding, soft-wrap interaction, IME composition, BiDi text\n269: \n270: **Impact**: Created systematic AMP_REPORT.md with prioritized fixes. Prevented 1-3h of debugging per issue.\n271: \n272: **Lesson Learned**: Always have oracle review reference docs before implementation - catches subtle algorithmic bugs\n273: that tests miss.\n274: \n275: </details>\n276: \n277: <details>\n278: <summary><strong>📚 Librarian: Keymap System Design</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-35b11d40-96b0-4177-9c75-4c723dfd8f80\">T-35b11d40</a></summary>\n279: \n280: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n281: \n282: **Research Question**: How do major editors implement configurable keyboard mappings?\n283: \n284: **Projects Studied**:\n285: | Editor | Pattern | Key Insight |\n286: |--------|---------|-------------|\n287: | VSCode | Flat vector + context | User overrides predictable via insertion order |\n288: | Helix | Trie-based | Efficient for prefix matching in modal editors |\n289: | Zed | Flat with depth indexing | Context depth + insertion order for precedence |\n290: | Neovim | Lua-based | Full scripting for complex mappings |\n291: \n292: **Key Finding**: Two dominant patterns - **trie-based** for modal editors (Helix) vs **flat vector with indexing** for\n293: context-rich editors (Zed, VSCode).\n294: \n295: **Outcome**: Created [docs/feature/KEYMAPPING.md](docs/feature/KEYMAPPING.md) design doc with data structures, TOML\n296: config format, and phased implementation plan.\n297: \n298: </details>\n299: \n300: <details>\n301: <summary><strong>🐛 Critical Bug: Cmd+Z Was Typing 'z'!</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-519a8c9d-b94f-45e5-98e0-5bfc34c77cbf\">T-519a8c9d</a></summary>\n302: \n303: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n304: \n305: **The Bug**: Undo/redo keyboard shortcuts were completely broken on macOS. Pressing Cmd+Z inserted the letter 'z' into\n306: the document instead of undoing!\n307: \n308: **Root Cause**: Key handler only checked `control_key()` modifier, not `super_key()` (macOS Command key).\n309: \n310: **The Fix**:\n311: \n312: ```rust\n313: // Before (broken on macOS)\n314: if modifiers.control_key() & & key == \"z\" { ... }\n315: \n316: // After (cross-platform)\n317: if (modifiers.control_key() | | modifiers.super_key()) & & key == \"z\" { ... }\n318: ```\n319: \n320: **Lesson Learned**: Always handle both `control_key()` AND `super_key()` for cross-platform keyboard shortcuts. The\n321: macOS Command key is a completely different modifier than Ctrl.\n322: \n323: </details>\n324: \n325: <details>\n326: <summary><strong>🏗️ Split View: EditorArea Architecture</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-29b1dd08-eee1-44fb-abd5-eb982d6bcd52\">T-29b1dd08</a></summary>\n327: \n328: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n329: \n330: **Problem**: Implement multi-pane editing with tabs, splits, and shared documents.\n331: \n332: **Architectural Decisions**:\n333: \n334: 1. **Shared documents, independent editors**: Documents stored in `HashMap<DocumentId, Document>`, editors in\n335:    `HashMap<EditorId, EditorState>`. Multiple editors can view the same document with independent cursors/viewports.\n336: \n337: 2. **Layout tree structure**: `LayoutNode` is either `Group(GroupId)` or `Split(SplitContainer)`. Recursive structure\n338:    allows arbitrary nesting.\n339: \n340: 3. **Migration helper**: `EditorArea::single_document()` provides backward compatibility:\n341: \n342: ```rust\n343: impl AppModel {\n344:     pub fn document(&self) -> &Document {\n345:         self.editor_area.focused_document()  // Hides complexity\n346:     }\n347: }\n348: ```\n349: \n350: **Lesson Learned**: Migration helpers like `single_document()` are crucial for phased refactoring. The pattern of \"\n351: replace two fields with one complex struct + accessors\" worked smoothly.\n352: \n353: </details>\n354: \n355: <details>\n356: <summary><strong>🖱️ Multi-Cursor: Only Primary Cursor Moved!</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-d4c75d42-c0c1-4746-a609-593bff88db6d\">T-d4c75d42</a></summary>\n357: \n358: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n359: \n360: **The Bug**: Arrow keys and all movement operations only affected the primary cursor. Secondary cursors were frozen!\n361: \n362: **Root Cause**: All movement handlers assumed single cursor - `move_cursor_up()` operated on `cursor_mut()` which only\n363: returned `cursors[0]`.\n364: \n365: **The Fix - Per-Cursor Primitives Pattern**:\n366: \n367: ```rust\n368: // Low-level: operates on one cursor\n369: fn move_cursor_left_at(&mut self, doc: &Document, idx: usize) { ... }\n370: \n371: // High-level: iterates all cursors\n372: fn move_all_cursors_left(&mut self, doc: &Document) {\n373:     for idx in 0..self.cursors.len() {\n374:         self.move_cursor_left_at(doc, idx);\n375:     }\n376: }\n377: ```\n378: \n379: **Critical Invariant**:\n380: \n381: ```rust\n382: // MUST maintain: cursors.len() == selections.len()\n383: // MUST maintain: cursors[i].to_position() == selections[i].head\n384: ```\n385: \n386: **Lesson Learned**: Always question single-item assumptions when adding multi-item support. The \"per-item primitive +\n387: all-items wrapper\" pattern scales well.\n388: \n389: </details>\n390: \n391: <details>\n392: <summary><strong>🔀 Selection Merging: merge_overlapping_selections()</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-e751be48-ab56-4b90-a196-d5df892d955b\">T-e751be48</a></summary>\n393: \n394: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n395: \n396: **The Problem**: After SelectWord with multiple cursors on adjacent words, selections would overlap and cause rendering\n397: bugs.\n398: \n399: **The Solution**: `merge_overlapping_selections()` maintains the parallel array invariant:\n400: \n401: ```rust\n402: pub fn merge_overlapping_selections(&mut self) {\n403:     // 1. Sort by start position\n404:     // 2. Merge consecutive overlapping/touching ranges\n405:     // 3. Update both cursors and selections arrays in lockstep\n406: }\n407: ```\n408: \n409: **Key Insight**: \"Touching\" selections like `[0,5)` and `[5,10)` should merge into `[0,10)` - matches user expectations\n410: for continuous selections.\n411: \n412: </details>\n413: \n414: <details>\n415: <summary><strong>📦 Module Extraction Sprint</strong> (7 threads, Dec 6)</summary>\n416: \n417: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n418: \n419: Systematic extraction from monolithic files:\n420: \n421: | Before      | After                                      | Lines            |\n422: | ----------- | ------------------------------------------ | ---------------- |\n423: | `main.rs`   | `app.rs`, `input.rs`, `view.rs`, `perf.rs` | 3100 → 20        |\n424: | `update.rs` | `update/` module directory (5 submodules)  | 2900 → organized |\n425: \n426: **Threads**: T-ce688bab → T-072af2cb (7 sequential extractions)\n427: \n428: **Result**: 669 tests passing, cleaner architecture, easier navigation.\n429: \n430: **Commits**: [`f602368`](https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/commit/f602368), [\n431: `71a3b87`](https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/commit/71a3b87)\n432: \n433: </details>\n434: \n435: <details>\n436: <summary><strong>🔬 Librarian: Zed GPUI Deep Dive</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-c764b2bc-4b0b-4a2a-8c65-c11460405741\">T-c764b2bc</a></summary>\n437: \n438: **Date**: 2025-12-07\n439: \n440: **Research Topic**: How does Zed's GPUI framework handle rendering and state?\n441: \n442: **Key Discoveries**:\n443: \n444: - **Three-phase render cycle**: `request_layout` → `prepaint` → `paint`\n445: - **Entity-based state**: Reference-counted handles with `notify()` for reactivity\n446: - **DispatchTree**: Hierarchical focus management and event routing\n447: - **Taffy layout engine**: Flexbox-style layout calculations\n448: \n449: **Patterns for Token**:\n450: \n451: - Modal/overlay system with hitbox blocking\n452: - Deferred draw for overlays\n453: - Focus trapping with hitbox system\n454: \n455: </details>\n456: \n457: ---\n458: \n459: <details>\n460: <summary><strong>📋 Full Thread Reference (58 threads)</strong></summary>\n461: \n462: All conversations are public. {
  "content": "[... omitted lines 1 to 59 ...]\n60: | ------------------- | -------------------------------------- |\n61: | `make test`         | Run all tests                          |\n62: | `make test-verbose` | Run tests with output                  |\n63: | `make test-fast`    | Run tests faster using `cargo nextest` |\n64: \n65: ---\n66: \n67: ## The Hidden Complexity of Text Editors\n68: \n69: You use a text editor every day. But do you actually understand what it's doing?\n70: \n71: On the surface, an editor is \"just\" text on a screen with a blinking cursor. Underneath, it's a small physics engine for\n72: glyphs, pixels, and input events—quietly solving dozens of problems every time you press a key.\n73: \n74: Consider a few things you rely on constantly but probably don't think about:\n75: \n76: - **Cursor + selection choreography** — Hold Shift and press arrow keys: the cursor moves, the selection grows or\n77:   shrinks, and the viewport sometimes nudges just enough to keep everything visible. Double-click a word, then\n78:   Shift+Click somewhere else. The rules feel obvious—until you try to implement them.\n79: \n80: - **Keyboard modifier edge cases** — What's the \"right\" behavior for Ctrl+Left on a line with emoji, snake_case, and\n81:   camelCase? What happens to your selection when you hit Cmd+Z, then Cmd+Shift+Z, then type? When should Alt extend\n82:   selection, Cmd jump by word, or Option+Option+Arrow add a new cursor?\n83: \n84: - **Glyphs that don't behave like characters** — A single \"character\" on screen might be multiple codepoints: combining\n85:   accents, zero-width joiners, ligatures. The cursor should snap to visually sensible positions, not halfway through a\n86:   grapheme cluster. Column count, byte offset, and rendered width all disagree—and the editor must reconcile them.\n87: \n88: - **Viewport scrolling that feels \"natural\"** — Page Down doesn't simply add `viewportHeight` to `scrollY`. It decides\n89:   which line becomes the new top, keeps the cursor in a \"safe zone,\" and handles partial lines at edges. Mouse wheel,\n90:   scrollbar drag, and \"Go to Line\" all produce subtly different scroll behavior—but never feel inconsistent when done\n91:   right.\n92: \n93: All of this hides behind a UI that's supposed to feel invisible. That's exactly why it's easy to underestimate the\n94: complexity.\n95: \n96: **Why this matters for AI-assisted development:** If you want AI agents to help design, debug, or extend an editor, they\n97: need more than \"there's a cursor and some lines.\" They need a precise, shared vocabulary for scroll offsets, viewports,\n98: cursor modes, selection semantics, and edge cases. The same is true for humans: explicit documentation is how you\n99: sanity-check your own assumptions and avoid rediscovering tricky behaviors through trial and error.\n100: \n101: Token is built around that idea: make geometry, behavior, and edge cases **fully explicit**—for both humans and\n102: machines. See [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) for our attempt to turn \"obvious\" editor behavior\n103: into a concrete, testable spec.\n104: \n105: ---\n106: \n107: ## AI-Assisted Development: A Framework for Complex Projects\n108: \n109: Token was built primarily through conversations with [Amp Code](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre). ## Assistant

All documentation is updated. Now let's discuss **Phase 2: Cell Editing**.

Looking at the design doc section on Phase 2 (lines 2002-2016 of csv-editor.md):

### Phase 2: Cell Editing - What's Required

#### Goals
Edit cells in place and sync changes back to the document buffer.

#### Core Deliverables

| Component | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| **CellEditState** | Tracks edit buffer, cursor position, original value for cancel/undo |
| **Cell editor rendering** | Show text cursor, input field inside cell |
| **Keyboard input** | Character insertion, backspace, arrow keys within cell |
| **Confirm/Cancel** | Enter confirms edit, Escape cancels and restores original |
| **Document sync** | Cell edit → rebuild CSV line → update document buffer |
| **Re-parse on external changes** | If document changes externally, re-parse CSV data |
| **Undo/redo integration** | Cell edits should be undoable via standard Cmd+Z |

#### Key Implementation Considerations

1. **Cell Edit Lifecycle:**
   - Press Enter or start typing → Enter edit mode
   - Show cursor in cell, handle keystrokes
   - Enter → Confirm, Escape → Cancel
   - Click elsewhere → Confirm current edit

2. **Document Sync Strategy:**
   - When edit is confirmed, reconstruct the entire line with proper escaping
   - Use the detected delimiter to rebuild
   - Handle quoting for values containing delimiter or newlines
   - Update the document's Rope buffer

3. **Undo Integration:**
   - Each cell edit should create an `EditOperation` for undo/redo
   - Consider batching rapid edits

4. **New Messages Needed:**
   ```
   CsvMsg::StartEditing
   CsvMsg::StartEditingWithChar(char)
   CsvMsg::ConfirmEdit
   CsvMsg::CancelEdit
   CsvMsg::EditInsertChar(char)
   CsvMsg::EditDeleteBackward
   CsvMsg::EditCursorLeft
   CsvMsg::EditCursorRight
   ```

5. **State Changes:**
   - Add `editing: Option<CellEditState>` to `CsvState`
   - `CellEditState` contains: position, buffer, cursor, original value

Would you like me to start implementing Phase 2, or do you have specific requirements or priorities for how cell editing should work?
